# Clock catalog

Generated by `mctas clock catalog` from `designs/clocks/archive/` (verdicts-v1.tsv, captions.tsv): never edit by hand. What a clock is and how it is judged: [docs/clocks.md](../../docs/clocks.md).

Legend: periods in game ticks with their real time (20 gt = 1 s). In the pictures (`mctas clock shot`, the real game), **orange glass = where the output signal is** (placed only for the picture, never part of the clock).

513 archived clocks, 513 distinct types (up to translation, rotation and mirror image), 70 families, 158 periods.

## Periods a family cannot reach

| family | periods | why |
|---|---|---|
| any redstone-tick family | 1, 2, 3 | A torch, repeater or comparator changes state (block state or comparator strength) only in its own scheduled tick, scheduled >= 2 gt ahead, and a position holds at most one pending tick of its block (LevelChunkTicks.schedule refuses a second (pos, block) pair): two changes of one component are >= 2 gt apart, so one on/off cycle takes >= 4 gt. Dust only follows them inside the tick. P 1, 2 or 3 needs a component changing twice within 2 gt. |
| any redstone-tick family, self-starting or kicked at an even tick | every odd P | Every delay is even (torch 2, comparator 2, repeater 2/4/6/8, burnout re-check 160, dust 0) and the placement's updates all happen between two ticks: every change happens at placement + even and pending ticks keep that parity, so the states at t and t + odd differ unless nothing is pending. Odd periods need a kick at an odd tick (`port start: ... at N`): P5 ring and P205 burnout exist that way. |
| any redstone-tick family containing redstone_torch | 4, 5, 6, 7, 8 | A torch in the active set turns off at least once per period; with P <= 8 its 8th turn-off comes within 7P <= 56 <= 60 gt of the first: RedstoneTorchBlock.isToggledTooFrequently burns it out and it stays dark >= 160 gt (re-check) -- not periodic with P. (Burnout clocks use exactly this: P220, P205.) |

## By period

### P = 4 gt (0.2 s): 5 types, 4 families

| picture | family | blocks | box | volume | duty | edges | start | judge | design | how it works |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| <img src="img/clk-rt-cmp-sub-4b.jpg" width="200"> | **comparator+redstone_wire** | 8 | 3x2x2 | 12 | 2/4 | 1 | self-start | robust, chiral | [clk-rt-cmp-sub-4b](archive/clk-rt-cmp-sub-4b.txt) | Comparator subtract clock, 8 blocks (draft, clk-rt): rear = redstone block (15); the output strongly powers block A; dust beside A carries A's strength to the comparator's side. Expected: output 15 / 1 alternating every 2 gt (period 4); the side dust 14 / 0. |
| <img src="img/clk-rt-cmp-sub-4.jpg" width="200"> | comparator+redstone_wire | 9 | 3x2x2 | 12 | 2/4 | 1 | self-start | robust, chiral | [clk-rt-cmp-sub-4](archive/clk-rt-cmp-sub-4.txt) | Comparator subtract clock (draft, clk-rt): rear = redstone block (15), output -> 3 dust -> side. Expected: output alternates 15 / 2 every 2 gt (period 4), the last dust 13 / 0. |
|  | **observer** | 4 | 1x2x3 | 6 | 2/4 | 1 | self-start | robust | [clk-obs-p4-obs-4b](archive/clk-obs-p4-obs-4b.txt) | CLOCK: census-k4-p4-0000 period 4 gt, on 4/4, edges 0, warmup 3, family observer, 4 blocks, 1x2x3, self-start (clk-obs, found by census-k4-p4-0000.txt; output: the changing cell with the most rising edges, declared so every rotation reads the same cell) |
| <img src="img/clk-rt-uloop-p4-rw-r-10b.jpg" width="200"> | **redstone_wire+repeater** | 10 | 3x2x2 | 12 | 2/4 | 1 | self-start | robust, chiral | [clk-rt-uloop-p4-rw-r-10b](archive/clk-rt-uloop-p4-rw-r-10b.txt) | U-loop (clk-rt): block A + dust beside it -> top row [delay-1 repeater (powered at placement)] -> block F -> dust beside F -> bottom row [delay-1 repeater] -> A; 0 torches = a pulse ring of loop delay 4 gt |
|  | **repeater** | 12 | 3x2x3 | 18 | 2/4 | 1 | self-start | robust, chiral | [clk-rt-ring3x3-p4-r-12b](archive/clk-rt-ring3x3-p4-r-12b.txt) | Rectangle ring (clk-rt): the 3x3 perimeter clockwise from the north-west corner = [b, 1*, b, 1, b, 1*, b, 1] (number = repeater delay, * = powered at placement, b = block, d = dust, T = wall torch on the block before it, c = comparator); 0 torches: a pulse ring of loop delay 8 gt |

### P = 8 gt (0.4 s): 8 types, 6 families

| picture | family | blocks | box | volume | duty | edges | start | judge | design | how it works |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| <img src="img/clk-sculk-c29-obs-stone-disp.jpg" width="200"> | calibrated_sculk_sensor+dispenser+observer | 4 | 4x1x2 | 8 | 10/29 | 1 | kick: redstone_block 1 gt at 3,0,1 | robust, chiral | [clk-sculk-c29-obs-stone-disp](archive/clk-sculk-c29-obs-stone-disp.txt) |  |
|  | **comparator+dropper+hopper+repeater** | 14 | 3x2x4 | 24 | 7/29 | 1 | self-start | robust, chiral | [droprep-p29](archive/droprep-p29.txt) | Dropper ring with a delayed fire (clock item sweep-dropring --delays): a hopper ring 4 or 5 deep with 1 or 2 droppers in the bottom row; the comparator in the inner column reads the top-row hopper above it and fires the dropper at the bottom through 1 or 2 repeaters (each on stone). Two stones: one waits in a dropper until the other passes the read hopper; the repeater delays (2 gt each step) shift the period off the multiples of 7, so ring size x dropper count x delays reach every P from 42 up (and 29-40 odd/even below). Here P = 29. redsim: P=29 gt, warmup 38, family comparator+dropper+hopper+repeater, 14 blocks, output on 7/29 gt, 1 rising edge(s) |

### P = 45 gt (2.25 s): 3 types, 3 families

| picture | family | blocks | box | volume | duty | edges | start | judge | design | how it works |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| <img src="img/clk-sculk-ring3-c45.jpg" width="200"> | **calibrated_sculk_sensor** | 7 | 16x1x17 | 272 | 10/45 | 1 | kick: use 0,0,0 | robust, chiral | [clk-sculk-ring3-c45](archive/clk-sculk-ring3-c45.txt) | Pure vibration ring (nothing but sensors changes state): calibrated sensors S1 (1,0,0), S2 (15,0,8), S3 (1,0,16), each with an amethyst block beside it; when S_i activates its amethyst resonates and S_(i+1) hears it floor(dist) = 15 gt later. P = 3 x 15. A1 is out of S3's range (dist^2 257), so after the kick only S2 wakes; afterwards the sensor two hops ahead is always still in its 20 gt dead time. Kick: right-click the note block K (only S1 is within 16 of it; S1 faces east so its back, K, gets no power from it). |
|  | **comparator+dropper+hopper+repeater** | 16 | 4x2x4 | 32 | 7/45 | 1 | self-start | robust, chiral | [droprep-p45](archive/droprep-p45.txt) | Dropper ring with a delayed fire (clock item sweep-dropring --delays): a hopper ring 4 or 5 deep with 1 or 2 droppers in the bottom row; the comparator in the inner column reads the top-row hopper above it and fires the dropper at the bottom through 1 or 2 repeaters (each on stone). Two stones: one waits in a dropper until the other passes the read hopper; the repeater delays (2 gt each step) shift the period off the multiples of 7, so ring size x dropper count x delays reach every P from 42 up (and 29-40 odd/even below). Here P = 45. redsim: P=45 gt, warmup 23, family comparator+dropper+hopper+repeater, 16 blocks, output on 7/45 gt, 1 rising edge(s) |
|  | **oak_fence_gate+observer+sculk_sensor** | 4 | 4x1x1 | 4 | 30/45 | 1 | self-start | robust, chiral | [clk-sculk-chain-p45-28](archive/clk-sculk-chain-p45-28.txt) | A plain sculk sensor, then observer, stone, gate beside, 3 from the sensor (travel time) how: chain plain line reps [], observer, stone, gate beside (travel 3) |

Two stones: one waits in a dropper until the other passes the read hopper. With L = 2w + 2 containers (L + 2 blocks): P = 7L - 29 with one dropper, 7L - 36 with two, so every P = 6 mod 7 from 20 up. |  | **calibrated_sculk_sensor+observer+sticky_piston** | 5 | 4x3x1 | 12 | 12/54 | 6 | self-start | robust, chiral | [clk-sculk-hyb-p54-3](archive/clk-sculk-hyb-p54-3.txt) | A calibrated sculk sensor watched by a chain of 2 observer(s) (each watching the previous); an oak trapdoor (placed open+powered: it closes on placement, the self-start) at the last output, 3 from the sensor (travel time): the observers' pulse after the sensor re-arms is heard; plus a sticky piston beside the chain (moves parts of it: the hybrid) how: obs cal k=2 path 1,0,0 2,0,0 emitter trapdoor at the output (travel 3) + sticky_piston[extended=false,facing=up] at 3,-1,0 |
| <img src="img/dropring-p54.jpg" width="200"> | **comparator+dropper+hopper** | 10 | 3x2x3 | 18 | 14/54 | 2 | self-start | robust, chiral | [dropring-p54](archive/dropring-p54.txt) | Dropper ring, 10 blocks (clk-item): hoppers and droppers around a 3x3 square, each passing items to the next; a comparator in the middle reads one ring container and points into the opposite one, a dropper: each time an item arrives under the comparator, the dropper fires 4 gt later (rising edge) and pushes one item on. Items wait in the droppers for a trigger, so the cycle is not a multiple of 7: 54 gt. redsim: P=54 gt, warmup 24, family comparator+dropper+hopper, 10 blocks, output on 14/54 gt, 2 rising edge(s) |

Two stones: one waits in a dropper until the other passes the read hopper; the repeater delays (2 gt each step) shift the period off the multiples of 7, so ring size x dropper count x delays reach every P from 42 up (and 29-40 odd/even below). Here P = 54. redsim: P=54 gt, warmup 23, family comparator+dropper+hopper+repeater, 16 blocks, output on 7/54 gt, 1 rising edge(s) |

|  | **redstone_torch+repeater** | 20 | 6x2x2 | 24 | 20/54 | 1 | self-start | robust, chiral | [clk-rt-uloop-T-1--1--3--4--4--4--4--4--T](archive/clk-rt-uloop-T-1--1--3--4--4--4--4--4--T.txt) | U-loop (clk-rt): a wall torch on block A -> top row [delay-1 repeater (powered at placement), delay-1 repeater (powered at placement), delay-3 repeater (powered at placement), delay-4 repeater (powered at placement)] -> block F -> a wall torch on F -> bottom row [delay-4 repeater (powered at placement), delay-4 repeater (powered at placement), delay-4 repeater (powered at placement), delay-4 repeater (powered at placement)] -> A; 2 torches = a pulse ring of loop delay 54 gt |

|  | **hopper+redstone_torch+redstone_wire** | 8 | 2x3x4 | 24 | 16/64 | 8 | self-start | robust, chiral | [hopburn-p64](archive/hopburn-p64.txt) | Burnout hybrid (found by clock item fill on the hopper pair): 2 swords slosh between 2 hoppers (one move per 8 gt) and the comparator reading the east-facing hopper, in compare mode, re-ticks on every 1-gt dip and updates the cell in front of it even when its output stays 3. That poke reaches a wall torch on stone whose dust (on the same stone) burns it out: 8 flips in 2-gt steps, then the 60-gt burnout window; the first poke after the window relights it. Torch period 64 = 8 hopper moves. redsim: P=64 gt, warmup 95, family hopper+redstone_wall_torch+redstone_wire, 8 blocks, output on 16/64 gt, 8 rising edge(s) |
| <img src="img/clk-rt-uloop-p64-rt-rw-r-13b.jpg" width="200"> | **redstone_torch+redstone_wire+repeater** | 13 | 4x2x2 | 16 | 32/64 | 1 | self-start | robust, chiral | [clk-rt-uloop-p64-rt-rw-r-13b](archive/clk-rt-uloop-p64-rt-rw-r-13b.txt) | U-loop (clk-rt): block A + dust beside it -> top row [delay-3 repeater (powered at placement), delay-4 repeater (powered at placement)] -> block F -> a wall torch on F -> bottom row [delay-4 repeater (powered at placement), delay-4 repeater (powered at placement)] -> A; 1 torch(es) = an inverter loop of delay 32 gt (period 2 x delay) |

### P = 65 gt (3.25 s): 3 types, 3 families

| picture | family | blocks | box | volume | duty | edges | start | judge | design | how it works |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
|  | **comparator+dropper+hopper+repeater** | 20 | 4x2x5 | 40 | 7/65 | 1 | self-start | robust, chiral | [droprep-p65](archive/droprep-p65.txt) | Dropper ring with a delayed fire (clock item sweep-dropring --delays): a hopper ring 4 or 5 deep with 1 or 2 droppers in the bottom row; the comparator in the inner column reads the top-row hopper above it and fires the dropper at the bottom through 1 or 2 repeaters (each on stone). Two stones: one waits in a dropper until the other passes the read hopper; the repeater delays (2 gt each step) shift the period off the multiples of 7, so ring size x dropper count x delays reach every P from 42 up (and 29-40 odd/even below). Here P = 65. redsim: P=65 gt, warmup 30, family comparator+dropper+hopper+repeater, 20 blocks, output on 7/65 gt, 1 rising edge(s) |
|  | **comparator+hopper+redstone_torch+redstone_wire** | 8 | 2x3x3 | 18 | 63/65 | 1 | self-start | robust, chiral | [hopburn-p65](archive/hopburn-p65.txt) | Burnout hybrid (found by clock item fill on the hopper pair): a wall torch on the stone next to the west-facing hopper, with a block above the torch and dust on the stone, burns out (8 flips, then the 60-gt window); while its dust is lit the stone locks that hopper, which shifts the 2-sword slosh and the comparator reading the other hopper by one move, so the comparator dips once per cycle and its update relights the torch: P = 65. redsim: P=65 gt, warmup 95, family comparator+hopper+redstone_wall_torch+redstone_wire, 8 blocks, output on 63/65 gt, 1 rising edge(s) |

Two stones: one waits in a dropper until the other passes the read hopper; the repeater delays (2 gt each step) shift the period off the multiples of 7, so ring size x dropper count x delays reach every P from 42 up (and 29-40 odd/even below). Here P = 103. redsim: P=103 gt, warmup 32, family comparator+dropper+hopper+repeater, 26 blocks, output on 7/103 gt, 1 rising edge(s) |

|  | **dropper+hopper+jukebox+note_block+redstone_torch+redstone_wire** | 9 | 2x3x2 | 12 | 6/1446 | 1 | self-start | robust, chiral | [jukebox9-p1446](archive/jukebox9-p1446.txt) | Jukebox carousel, 9 blocks (clk-item): 3 copies of disc 11 go around a jukebox J. A playing jukebox emits 15: it locks the extractor hopper below it, powers the inserter dropper beside it, keeps the torch standing on it off, and lights a 2-dust line whose second dust sits on a note block next to the lift dropper. When the song ends (lengthInTicks + 20 gt) the extractor pushes its waiting disc into the lift and pulls the played one; the torch relights 2 gt later and fires the inserter through the cell above it (4 gt): the next disc goes in. The new song lights the dust, the note block flips and fires the lift 4 gt later: the waiting disc rises into the inserter. Period = song + 6 gt, whatever the hopper tick order. |

When the song ends (lengthInTicks + 20 gt) the extractor pushes its waiting disc into the lift and pulls the played one; the torch relights 2 gt later and fires the inserter through the cell above it (4 gt): the next disc goes in.
